# Grant Checklist 
- [ ] Start the Google doc (see the following [template](https://docs.google.com/document/d/13-fR-bRiDEotzHWIY_w08Kdul4s0NyjsHOl7sqqXNDc/edit?tab=t.0) for reference)
  - [ ] Copypaste the standard tables from previous grants (see template) and replace the basic grant information (deadline, award amount, \# of awards etc) with the new grant information as found in the RFP.
  - [ ] Create a TOC.
  - [ ] Below the tables, include the following categories: Questions, Action Items, Approach, Meetings, Narrative, and Budget (The budget section is merely a link to a Google Sheet) 
  - [ ] Make sure these sections correspond to tabs that are visible in the lefthand column of the doc
  - [ ] Make sure the doc is “pageless” (See File\>Page Setup for that setting). This allows the categories to be collapsible. 
- [ ] Read carefully through the RFP
  - [ ] Read through it again
    - [ ] Identify any confusing parts
    - [ ] Identify the eligibility requirements
      - [x] Rarely, they let forprofit companies apply as the Lead
      - [ ] Most of the time, we apply on behalf of a nonprofit or CBO as technical assistants
        - [ ] If the latter, identify the partner or partners with whom we could apply
- [ ] Add grant structure explicitly into the Google Doc.
  - [ ] Turn the scoring criteria into a blank application by turning the statements of the scoring criteria into questions 
    - [ ] If the application already comes as a set of questions, then creatively embed the scoring criteria within the blank questions   
- [ ] **Grant Lift & Approach**
  - [ ] Develop strategy
    - [ ] Estimate the lift and budget and resources to produce the grant
    - [ ] Calendar Max and Merissas time and critical deadlines from now til grant deadline.
      - [ ] If you have short term proposed needs for other staff first include them on your calendar for review.
    - [ ] Identify Hyphae staff that are needed to work on the grant and why you think so and when: 
      - [ ] *MV, IH, JPG, MM etc… *
      - [ ] *use BB or DF sparingly for strategy and content (not forms)*
  - [ ] Don't proceed until you Present Strategy to BB 
    - [ ] If you can't meet with brent, show him the calendar and strategy in narrative form.
    - [ ] If brent is not available to review strategy virtually or in a meeting,
      - [ ] Turn to DF
      - [ ] IH if DF is out
  - [ ] Transfer the the lift in ClickUp & Calendar
    - [ ] Add subtasks in checklist form
    - [ ] Calendar out other staff
- [ ] Develop a draft of an approach
  - [ ] Try to meet with Brent to develop approach
  - [ ] If he is not available, share that approach with Brent and if he approves, record his suggested approach
  - [ ] Synthesize that approach and paste it into the Narrative section of the doc as well as in the Approach section (which is an abridged version)      
  - [ ] Contact partners to gauge interest and schedule meetings
  - [ ] Start pulling in previous grant material  
  - [ ] Assign sections of the proposal to each person: budget, technical sections, community engagement narrative etc
  - [ ] Calendar out when their sections need to be completed
  - [ ] Gather all relevant questions from team internally..then
    - [ ] Email grant administrator with any questions (which are then listed for reference in the Questions section)
- [ ] Simultaneously with Narrative development, you will have started the Budget 
  - [ ] Assess grant length
  - [ ] Pull budgets from AdaptOS and other relevant budgets
  - [ ] Determine basic element of scope (PM, Monitoring, Modeling, Community Engagement etc)
  - [ ] First price out the PM costs
  - [ ] Based on what’s left over after PM, price out the remaining budget elements
  - [ ] If applying with a CBO, make sure that the CBO share of the budget exceeds the Hyphae share
  - [ ] Determine how much the CBO staff wants to be paid
    - [ ] Are there benchmarks for this? (Scale: $40/hr  ?)   
- [ ] Collect Letters of Support/Commitment
- [ ] Collect required forms 
- [ ] Get CBO partners to write their sections of the Narrative
  - [ ] If they do not have capacity, ask them to submit information about their org, its legacy, and why they are best suited to be selected for the award
- [ ] Have Brent and Fletch review the document
  - [ ] Make revisions
- [ ] Have Brent and Fletch review again
  - [ ] Make revisions
- [ ] Throughout the process, upload proposal components into a Google Drive folder
  - [ ] Either merge into a single PDF or gather as Google Drive folder of distinct files, as grant guidelines indicate
- [ ] Get Final Approval from BB and DF
- [ ] Previously assign a person to compile all the grant elements into either a folder (with separate elements) or a combined PDF
- [ ] Previously assign a person to be in charge of submitting the grant.  
- [ ] Submit  
# Memento Tattoos
(Inspired by the Guy Pierce character in the Christopher Nolan movie, these are things to tattoo on your chest about grantwriting that you forget over and over despite your best efforts to remember them)
1.  Start the budget early 
2.  Start pulling in previous grant material before writing anything
3.  Don’t allow the process of collecting forms and LoS derail the writing of the Narrative and Budget 
4.  DELEGATE, GODDAMN IT, DON’T BE A HERO 
5.  Stay on the partners to get them to agree to a budget right away
Time Table Best Practices 
46 weeks: 
Prioritize partners, letters of support/forms, then budget, then narrative
3 weeks or less: 
Prioritize budget, partners/letters/forms (requires at least two dedicated days from partners), then narrative

**MP to Christine Viterelli **
**12/20/24**
**Subject:** Follow-Up on Arvin/Lamont RFP and Meeting Summary
Dear Christine,
Thank you again for your time today. We’re excited about the opportunity to collaborate on the Arvin/Lamont RFP and beyond. Below is a summary of our discussion and the materials we’ll need to proceed.
My understanding was that you’re willing to sign off as Lead Applicant on this Arvin/Lamont application so long as we do the work. That’s fine – that’s often how we operate\!  
  - The most practical way to approach the proposal is to integrate vegetative barrier plantings into the ongoing sidewalk, curb, and gutter projects that are currently in design stages, particularly along the eastern side of Comanche, which is within city jurisdiction.
  - Contributing to ongoing sidewalk improvements may allow for planting in areas adjacent to or behind the sidewalks, such as drainage ditches, to maximize effectiveness within the funding constraints.
  - Limited funding on Comanche can fund a pilot block, paving the way for larger vegetative barrier projects.
    1.  Residents may expect large-scale planting, but limited funding requires setting clear expectations about the scope and pilot nature of the project
  - Inspired by our Stockton veg buff project, we opted to pitch a general proposal to the Air District, with details to be refined after securing the grant, similar to our approach in Stockton.
  - Your preference is for drought-tolerant native species and shrubs with manageable root systems to avoid disrupting sidewalks, but you are flexible. 
Strategic Opportunities
  - Looking to the future, we also explored pursing other transformative grants for infrastructure, tree planting, and vegetative barriers, particularly those that addressed long-term maintenance challenges.
  - Building on past successes, such as in Stockton, this project could demonstrate feasibility and attract larger funding opportunities.
Maintenance and Community Engagement
  - We are also aligned in wanting to:
      - Leverage **stewardship funds** with external partners for long-term upkeep aligns with our approach.
      - Build up programs like "Tree City" or adopt-a-tree initiatives that could engage the community in maintaining vegetation, especially for future projects.
      - Partner with with local contractors, such as **Clean Cut**, for maintenance and planting.
Materials and Contacts Requested
To proceed, we’d appreciate the following:
  - A **map of city-controlled boundaries**, including parks, schools, and right-of-way areas.
  - Contact information for:
      - **Sam Lux**, City Engineer, regarding sidewalk and AB 617 project coordination.
      - **Yolanda Alcantar**, Kern County Engineer, for the western Comanche project.
  - Access to Arvin’s **tree inventory and plan** to align with existing goals.
  - Contextual information on sites like **Bear Mountain Elementary School**, **Arvin High School**, and the **new community college campus**.
  - Templates or prior **letters of support** relevant to the application.

# Letters

**\[Insert Letterhead\]

**San Joaquin Valley Air Pollution Control District  
**Attn: Grants and Incentives  
1990 East Gettysburg Avenue  
Fresno, CA 93726-0244  
grants@valleyair.org

**Subject:** Arvin/Lamont Community Emissions Reduction Program – Vegetative Barriers
 
Dear Grant Administrator,
 
Please accept this letter of support for Hyphae Design Lab’s vegetated barrier proposal for Arvin and Lamont. If the RFP is accepted, the City of Arvin (henceforward, “the City”) authorizes Hyphae to plant on City-owned land.
 
To guarantee maximum alignment and cost-benefit, the exact planting sites will be finalized post-award. If the RFP is awarded, Hyphae will first collaborate with city engineers to understand project overlaps. Hyphae will then identify specific areas that are likely to be the most effective locations for the vegetated barriers. They will also interface with the Air District and with AB 617 stakeholders and the community to get input and feedback.
 
If timelines, logistics, and engineering align, Hyphae will collaborate with City engineers to incorporate the vegetated barriers into the City’s ongoing road and sidewalk improvements along Comanche and Tejon, which are already supported by AB 617 funding. The City will also provide maps of other City-controlled areas, and provide appropriate contacts at the County of Kern, Kern School District, and the recently completed community college, if appropriate. 
 
Hyphae has experience collaborating with both municipalities as well as other AB617 communities like Stockton, Oakland and Richmond to implement vegetated barriers, and we look forward to collaborating with them on this project. 
 
The City of Arvin is proud to support this project as a vital step toward achieving our community's AB 617 goals. Building on our recently completed urban forest plan, we see this pilot project as an opportunity to model innovative green infrastructure solutions to air quality challenges in our community and the broader Central Valley region.
 
Please contact me at \[phone number\] or \[email\] if you have any questions.
 
Sincerely,  
  
\[Signature\]  
\[Name\]  
\[Title\]
Email
Dear Christine, 
Happy new year\! I hope the holiday season was restful and restorative. Thanks for agreeing to support this RFP submission with a letter of support. We know you’re very busy and will try to make it as small a time lift for you as possible.
Attached you will find a drafted letter of support for the SJVAir vegetated barrier grant.
The grant is due January 9th at 4PM PT. We know this is a tight timeline but it would be great if we could have the letter back by Wednesday the 8th. 
We’re still in the process of developing the details and budget but I've also included a link to the preliminary draft of the application.  
A few hopefully quick requests:
1.  Regarding the ongoing road improvements that we hope to dovetail with: 
  - Do you have any conceptual plans on hand that we can try to conform our design to? No problem if not – we are happy to draw something up.
  - Do you have a workplan and timeline for that project, so we can try to align the vegetated barrier work plan and proposal to it? 
  - If it's easiest, can you send us what you proposed to the State and we can pull the above info out?
2\) Regarding water supply for irrigation, we are hopeful the project will have water for irrigation but we think we should include a budget for water supply if not as a contingency. Would water come from the city? From the local irrigation district? Is there a good contact for this? Or would that person also be Sam who is out.
3\) Finally\! We are also identifying some local landscape subcontractors. We have been asking around, but we're also wondering if there are any that are already approved to do work for the city? Or if you have any landscape maintenance that you work with who are on call? If awarded, we will likely put it out to bid but it would be good to know if there's any that already work with the city.
If it's faster to tell me answers to any of these questions, I'm always more than happy to just talk, whatever is easiest for you\!
Wishing you my very best,
Max

**\[Date\]

Fresno, CA 93726-0244  
<grants@valleyair.org>
**Subject:** Arvin/Lamont Community Emissions Reduction Program – Vegetative Barriers
 
<comment_start id=kix.1y2gyuyon0w4>Dear Grant Administrator,**<comment_end id=kix.1y2gyuyon0w4>**
I am pleased to provide this letter of support for the vegetated barrier proposal being developed by Hyphae Design Lab in partnership with the City of Arvin. Our team at Platinum Pines is excited about the opportunity to contribute to this important project, which aims to integrate vegetative barriers into urban infrastructure to mitigate air pollution and improve public health outcomes. Please note that the numbers provided in this letter are provisional and subject to adjustment based on final site conditions, tree species availability, and project requirements.
**Tree Planting and Irrigation Installation**:
  - We will provide labor and equipment to plant approximately 125 trees in 24-inch and 36-inch boxes, with a per-tree cost range of $1,300–$1,800 for labor and irrigation installation.
  - We are equipped with excavators and skid steerers to handle planting logistics efficiently and effectively.
**Coordination with Tree Suppliers**:
  - We will collaborate with tree nurseries to ensure the highest quality trees are sourced. If tasked with tree procurement, we will offer a three-month establishment warranty to ensure tree health and proper planting.
**Establishment Maintenance**:
  - We can also provide establishment maintenance services for the first three years. This includes irrigation system checks, structural pruning, and replacement of up to 15% of trees as needed.
**Flexibility and Collaboration**:
  - We understand that site-specific logistics and tree species availability will impact final decisions. Our team is ready to adapt to changing conditions and provide updated cost estimates based on precise project parameters.
Platinum Pines is enthusiastic about supporting this transformative project and helping create sustainable green infrastructure in the City of Arvin. We look forward to collaborating with Hyphae Design Lab and other stakeholders to achieve the project’s goals.
Please feel free to contact me directly at \[Email Address\] or \[Phone Number\] for any additional details or documentation.
Sincerely,
**\[Name\]  
**Platinum Pines  
\[Title\]  
\[Contact Information\]

# Rough notes

Rough Notes Hyphae x Caltrans 
4/30/25
  - Form a PDT combining District 6 with District 10 so that they can get up to speed about these innovative practices
      - Way to soften District 6 about interventions in the ROW to which they will be highly averse
  - BB: Caltrans standards vary district to district, so perhaps not as atypical as it sounds 
      - In Stocketon: worked with a whole range of depts that were present to finally settle on Adopt-a-Highway as a permitting strategy 
      - Option 1: Qu: Do we form a PDT combining Districts 10 and 6?
          - Option 2: **Or,** do we just choose a road and submit an adopt-a-highway form for a given road? More of a box check?
          - JA happy to endorse that approach 
              - Would need to address irrigation issues and other things
          - Previous Caltrans discussions had said A-a-H would be fine unless project gets too complicated and actually requires an encroachment permit 
              - But that encroachment permit is much easier to get for a 5% part of the project
              - Rather than going through the whole bureaucratic rigamarole of starting a whole new Caltrans project, or even option 2, which is encroachment.  
              - JA: Still need to address concrete removal and maintenance issues     
              - BB: And we can include the coordination w/ District 10 at a later date to ease their fears 
              - BB: possibly submit A-a-H form for both Arvin-Lamont and Shafter?
                  - Revisit re: upcoming meeting
              - JA: I can support by explaining the benefits and giving you credit for cracking the code for community benefit 
                  - Conforms to new Caltrans mission 
              - Write “fact sheet”
                  - We’re bringing a solution not a problem 
                  - We’re handling the maintenance – find the water etc
                  - Show that you have success in a similarly arid district (e.g. success of being awarded the Stockton project in District 10) 
                  - And that this was the advice of District 10 landscape architects    
                  - Show that we know about Caltrans needs (refer to transcript)
                  - Show that we have the solution 
                  - Names of Caltrans landscape architects and others 
                  - Include success stories of Diana Gomez and Tree Fresno as precedent (Retired in December; Michael Navarro her successor since February)
              - Next Shafter meeting May 13th 
                  - City of Shafter submitting to put a barrier around the school
                  - Unclear how much they asked for
                  - Shafter urgent
                  - Arvin-Lamont can take more time to process and vet sites

# Air District Letter

\[Hyphae Letterhead or Email Signature Block\]
\[Date\]
To: \[Valley Air District contact name, if known\]  
San Joaquin Valley Air Pollution Control District
Re: Advancing Implementation, Maintenance, and Planning Support for Vegetative Buffer Projects in Arvin-Lamont and Shafter
<comment_start id=kix.kxt1wrd0u498>Dear<comment_end id=kix.kxt1wrd0u498> \[Valley Air District team / specific name\],
I am writing to share Hyphae’s strategy for implementing vegetated barriers in Arvin, Lamont, and Shafter, and to propose three coordinated investments essential to advancing Valley Air’s environmental justice and air quality goals:
1.  Pilot Project Implementation
2.  Maintenance Endowment
3.  Integrated Planning Support
I. *Pilot Projects – Arvin and Shafter Implementation, Lamont Expansion*
In addition to our partnership with the City of Arvin, we are now coordinating closely with Kern County and Caltrans District 6 to implement vegetated barriers across all three communities.
In Lamont, we are requesting $500,000 in additional funding. Based on our calculations, the initial RFP scope is insufficient to support two fully developed, well-designed vegetated barriers in both Arvin and Lamont. We’ve identified a clear path forward with Caltrans for a barrier along Route 184 near Lamont Elementary School—one of the community’s top-priority sites—with other candidate sites also under evaluation. To streamline permitting, we are advancing this work through the Adopt-A-Highway model successfully used in our Stockton project. Our Caltrans partner has offered to present this approach at the upcoming AB 617 meetings for both Lamont and Shafter to build cross-agency support.
In Arvin, our lead site is along Comanche Road, adjacent to Bear Mountain Elementary School—a location prioritized by residents due to pesticide drift exposure. The City of Arvin has assured us that they can integrate our landscape and irrigation scope into their ongoing AB 617-funded sidewalk and curb improvements, thus minimizing construction burden, leveraging public dollars, and ensuring local coordination. Should the community not support that site, we are prepared to present alternative locations at the upcoming subcommittee meeting.
In Shafter, we are coordinating with Caltrans to pursue a vegetated barrier along a community-prioritized section of Route 43, with other sites under evaluation. This work will also advance under the Adopt-A-Highway model, which streamlines permitting and provides a replicable approach for similar communities.
We are also working with Kern County to assess greening opportunities along County-managed corridors in both Lamont and Shafter, where several AB 617 and ARPA-funded infrastructure projects are underway. In Lamont, these include East Panama Road, Buena Vista Boulevard, and Mountain View Road. In Shafter, relevant projects include the Mexican Colony sidewalk program and a proposed bike lane network on Burbank Street and North Shafter Avenue.
Kern County has made clear, however, that it cannot support additional greening elements without a viable solution for long-term care—a broader structural challenge facing many small jurisdictions, which we aim to address through the endowment strategy outlined below.
II. *Long-Term Maintenance – Endowment Model*
A recurring barrier to green infrastructure implementation is the absence of dedicated operations and maintenance (O\&M) funding. County staff have cited multiple examples—such as the Northchester median trees—where investments failed due to irrigation breakdowns and no available repair budget, ultimately leaving the County responsible for costly removals.
To address this, our submitted budgets allocate 40% toward long-term care, forming the foundation for a regional maintenance endowment—potentially managed by the San Joaquin Community Foundation or a similar entity. Our goal is to build this fund to $1 million over time, enabling sustained support for vegetated barrier projects across multiple AB 617 zones and de-risking long-term commitments for partners like Kern County, Caltrans, and private landowners.
This model directly addresses a structural tension in the RFP: a 3-year implementation window paired with a 10-year maintenance requirement, but no clear path to fund the latter. We welcome Valley Air’s partnership in exploring ways to scale the endowment—whether through supplemental investment or matched contributions—to provide durable O\&M solutions across the region.
III. *Masterplanning and Design Integration*
Through the process of implementing these pilot projects, we’ve identified a broader gap: many of the hundreds of infrastructure projects currently funded through AB 617 (e.g., sidewalks, bike lanes, pedestrian corridors) do not include tree planting, shaded zones, or vegetative buffers—despite being sited in high-exposure communities.
We propose a third strategic investment: $500,000 to support integrated planning and design work in coordination with local cities and counties. These agencies are often overburdened and understaffed. With modest planning support, we could help embed tree wells, planters, and irrigation systems into their ongoing engineering work, even if funding for full greening implementation isn’t yet available.
Such design-forward collaboration would enable Valley Air to maximize the co-benefits of existing infrastructure investments—including shade, heat mitigation, and pollutant filtration—while institutionalizing a more holistic model for EJ-driven capital planning.
In conclusion, these three coordinated approaches—pilot implementation, long-term maintenance via endowment, and integrated planning support—represent a durable and scalable model for vegetated barrier development. This is not simply a request for project approval, but a strategy grounded in interagency collaboration and community-driven priorities, designed for lasting impact.
We’d welcome the opportunity to work together on a path forward and are happy to provide fact sheets and draft materials in advance of the upcoming AB 617 meetings in Shafter (May 12) and Arvin-Lamont (May 28). Thank you for your continued partnership and your commitment to building healthier, more resilient communities across the Valley.
Sincerely,
  
Brent Bucknum  
Hyphae Design Lab  
brent@hyphae.net  
cc: Max Pingeon, Christine Viterelli, Sam Lux, Yolanda Alcantar, Jason Adair
\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_
Qu of ask:
  - Calc the extra maintenance and ask to release an RFP?
      - Variant on urban greening follow-up RFP in Stockton 
      - Pitch it to the community   
      - But emphasize the urgency: we can’t miss a window 
Respond and give windows:
  - Don’t need to price out a buffer
  - Need a broad window of pricing to scale the buffer
